
FILE core/src/main/kotlin/com/jeevavibeapp/spendwise/core/Parsing.kt
1: package com.jeevavibeapp.spendwise.core
2: 
3: import java.text.Normalizer
4: import java.time.LocalDate
5: import java.time.LocalDateTime
6: 
7: /**
8:  * Deterministic SMS / UPI transaction parser. Pure Kotlin, no Android.
9:  *
10:  * A direct port of the Python parser this app used to embed, tuned against
11:  * real Indian bank and UPI formats: HDFC, SBI ("debited by 199.0", no Rs
12:  * prefix), ICICI ("; SWIGGY credited."), Axis ("UPI/P2M/<ref>/ZOMATO/…"),
13:  * Kotak ("Sent Rs.20.00 from Kotak Bank AC X1234 to swiggy8@ybl"), PhonePe,
14:  * GPay, Paytm, card alerts and EMI debits.
15:  *
16:  * Deliberately has no dependency on Android, Room or any framework, so it can
17:  * be compiled and tested on a plain JVM. That is not a stylistic preference:
18:  * the whole value of this file is the edge cases encoded in it, and those are
19:  * only worth anything if they can be re-verified cheaply.
20:  */
21: object Parsing {
22: 
23:     /** Bumped whenever matching behaviour changes, so a parse miss can be
24:      *  attributed to the parser that produced it and re-checked after a fix. */
25:     const val PARSER_VERSION = "2026.1.0-kt"
26: 
27:     /** Upper bound on a single transaction, in rupees. Absurdly generous (one
28:      *  lakh crore) on purpose — the job is not to second-guess the user, it is
29:      *  to guarantee the value is finite and survives (amount * 100) without
30:      *  overflowing, which every downstream money calculation assumes. */
31:     const val MAX_AMOUNT = 1e12
32: 
33:     private const val D = "$"          // '$' opens a template inside a raw string
34: 
35:     // Indian-script digits seen in regional-language bank SMS. NFKC folds most
36:     // of them, but these are mapped explicitly so amounts survive.
37:     private val DIGIT_MAP: Map<Char, Char> = buildMap {
38:         val scripts = listOf('\u0966', '\u0BE6', '\u0C66')   // Deva, Tamil, Telugu
39:         for (base in scripts) for (i in 0..9) put(base + i, ('0' + i))
40:     }
41: 
42:     // Zero-width and directional marks that banks and telcos inject, and that
43:     // silently break otherwise-correct patterns.
44:     private val INVISIBLE = Regex("[\u200b-\u200f\u202a-\u202e\u2060\ufeff]")
45:     private val WS_RUN = Regex("[ \t]+")
46: 
47:     private const val CURRENCY = "(?:rs\\.?|inr|₹)"
48:     private const val NUM = "([0-9][0-9,]*(?:\\.[0-9]{1,2})?)"
49: 
50:     private val I = setOf(RegexOption.IGNORE_CASE)
51: 
52:     /** Amount with an explicit currency marker: "Rs.450.00", "INR 1,234". */
53:     private val AMOUNT = Regex("$CURRENCY\\s*$NUM", I)
54: 
55:     /** SBI-style verb-anchored amount with no currency marker:
56:      *  "A/C X9218 debited by 199.0", "credited with 5,000". */
57:     private val VERB_AMOUNT = Regex(
58:         "\\b(?:debited|credited)\\s+(?:by|with|for|of)\\s+(?:$CURRENCY\\s*)?$NUM", I)
59: 
60:     /** Amounts that are a balance, not the transaction: "Avl Bal Rs 12,430.50". */
61:     private val BALANCE_CTX = Regex(
62:         "(?:avl|avail(?:able)?|a/c|account|total|closing|updated)\\s*(?:bal(?:ance)?)?\\s*" +
63:         "(?:bal(?:ance)?)?\\s*(?:is|:|-)?\\s*$D", I)
64: 
65:     private val REF = Regex(
66:         "(?:ref(?:erence)?(?:\\s*(?:no|number|id))?|txn(?:\\s*id)?|utr|upi\\s*ref)" +
67:         "[:\\s.#-]*([A-Za-z0-9]{6,})", I)
68: 
69:     private val CREDIT = Regex("\\b(credited|received|deposit(?:ed)?)\\b", I)
70:     private val DEBIT = Regex("\\b(debited|spent|paid|sent|withdrawn|purchase(?:d)?)\\b", I)
71: 
72:     /** A refund names the payment it undoes — "for your order paid via
73:      *  UPI" — so it carries a debit verb that is about the past. Only these
74:      *  two say money is leaving in this message. */
75:     private val REFUND = Regex("\\brefund(?:ed)?\\b", I)
76:     private val DEBITED_NOW = Regex("\\b(debited|withdrawn)\\b", I)
77: 
78:     /** The transactional gate: a money-movement verb must be present, or the
79:      *  message is promotional or informational and must never be captured. */
80:     internal val TXN_VERB = Regex(
81:         "\\b(debited|credited|spent|paid|sent|received|withdrawn|purchase(?:d)?|" +
82:         "deposit(?:ed)?|transferred|payment)\\b", I)
83: 
84:     /** Money that has NOT moved: offers, UPI collect requests, autopay
85:      *  pre-debit reminders, EMI notices, declined and reversed transactions,
86:      *  and the marketing / lending / scam vocabulary that carries amounts and
87:      *  sometimes even transaction verbs. */
88:     internal val NON_TXN = Regex(
89:         "(\\boff\\b|\\boffer|cashback|discount|coupon|% ?off|flat \\d|" +
90:         "payment request|requested|collect request|is requesting|" +
91:         "will be (?:debited|deducted|charged)|due on|due by|overdue|" +
92:         "e-?mandate|autopay.{0,20}(?:scheduled|upcoming)|" +
93:         "declined|failed|reversed|refund initiated|otp|one.?time password|" +
94:         "credit score|cibil|pre-?approved|pre-?qualified|eligible for|" +
95:         "loan offer|personal loan|instant loan|apply now|click here|" +
96:         "congratulations|you have won|claim now|lucky (?:winner|draw)|" +
97:         "limited period|hurry|t&c apply|terms and conditions apply|" +
98:         "unsubscribe|click .{0,12}(?:link|bit\\.ly|tinyurl)|bit\\.ly|tinyurl|" +
99:         "verify (?:your )?kyc|kyc (?:update|pending|expired)|will be blocked|" +
100:         "account will be (?:blocked|suspended|closed)|" +
101:         "bill (?:is )?generated|statement (?:is )?generated|minimum amount due|" +
102:         "total amount due|outstanding|emi of|recharge|plan validity|" +
103:         "interest rate|low interest|no documents|approved)", I)
104: 
105:     /** Positive evidence of a REAL bank/UPI transaction: essentially every
106:      *  genuine alert cites an account, card, UPI handle or reference.
107:      *  Promotional and scam messages carry amounts but almost never this, so
108:      *  requiring it is the highest-precision signal available offline. */
109:     internal val ACCOUNT_EVIDENCE = Regex(
110:         "(a/c|a/c no|acct|account\\s*(?:no|number|xx|\\*|ending)|" +
111:         "\\bac\\b\\s*[xX*\\d]|card\\s*(?:no\\.?\\s*)?[xX*\\d]|" +
112:         "\\bupi\\b|\\bvpa\\b|@[a-z]{2,}|" +
113:         "ref(?:erence)?\\s*(?:no|number|id)?[:\\s.#-]*[A-Za-z0-9]{6,}|" +
114:         "\\butr\\b|\\btxn\\b|transaction\\s*id|\\bimps\\b|\\bneft\\b|\\brtgs\\b|" +
115:         "[xX*]{2,}\\d{3,}|\\d{4,}\\b(?=\\s*(?:on|dated)))", I)
116: 
117:     /** Strings that are obviously not a merchant: bare dates, call-to-action
118:      *  verbs, and marketing phrases the payee pattern can latch onto. */
119:     private val BAD_MERCHANT = Regex(
120:         "^(?:\\d{1,2}[-/][A-Za-z]{3,9}[-/]\\d{2,4}|\\d{1,2}[-/]\\d{1,2}[-/]\\d{2,4}|" +
121:         "\\d[\\d,.]*|" +
122:         "(?:proceed|continue|click|apply|claim|verify|confirm|know more|" +
123:         "activate|register|download|login|update|check|call|contact|" +
124:         "low interest|no documents|improve.*|your .*score.*|avail.*|get .*)" +
125:         ")$D", I)
126: 
127:     /** The account the money moved through, standing where a payee should be:
128:      *  "debited from A/c XX1234 on …", "credited to your A/c XX9012". A
129:      *  message like that names no payee, and filing it under "A/c XX1234"
130:      *  would put every payee-less debit from that account under one merchant,
131:      *  so a single confirmation would teach the engine to file all of them
132:      *  the same way. */
133:     private val ACCOUNT_REF = Regex(
134:         "^(?:your|a/c|acct|account)\\b|(?:^|\\s)[xX*]+\\d{3,}\\b", I)
135: 
136:     // "ref\\w*" rather than a bare "ref": the original stopped only at the exact
137:     // word "ref", so "trf to SWIGGY Refno 553201998877" yielded the merchant
138:     // name "SWIGGY Refno 553201998877" — reference digits and all — which then
139:     // became its own merchant in the ledger and never matched the real SWIGGY.
140:     // "from" belongs here for the same reason "ref" does: without it,
141:     // "debited to ZOMATO from a/c **1234" yields the merchant "ZOMATO from",
142:     // which then becomes a separate merchant in the ledger from ZOMATO and
143:     // never matches the learning the user already did.
144:     private const val BOUNDARY =
145:         "(?=\\s+(?:on|ref\\w*|txn|utr|upi|avl|a/c|bal|info|via|using|to|from|not|is)\\b|[.,;]|$D)"
146: 
147:     /** Payee markers for debits. "from" is the payer side and is only trusted
148:      *  for credits — except as a last resort, because Kotak writes
149:      *  "Sent Rs.20 from Kotak Bank AC X1234 to swiggy8@ybl". */
150:     private val TO = Regex(
151:         "(?:\\bto\\b|\\bat\\b|towards|\\bvpa\\b|info[:\\-])\\s*" +
152:         "([A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9 &._@/-]{1,60}?)$BOUNDARY", I)
153:     private val FROM = Regex(
154:         "\\bfrom\\b\\s*([A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9 &._@/-]{1,60}?)$BOUNDARY", I)
155: 
156:     /** Axis card/UPI path: "UPI/P2M/519023481234/ZOMATO/…". */
157:     private val UPI_PATH = Regex("UPI/(?:P2[MA]/)?\\d{6,}/([A-Za-z0-9 &._-]{2,40})", I)
158: 
159:     /** ICICI style: "…; SWIGGY credited." names the payee before the verb. */
160:     private val PAYEE_CREDITED = Regex(
161:         "[;.]\\s*([A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9 &._-]{1,40}?)\\s+credited", I)
162: 
163:     private val DATE_PATTERNS = listOf(
164:         // "on date 08Jul26" is ordinary SBI phrasing and the original required
165:         // the digits to follow "on" immediately, so those messages silently
166:         // lost their date and were filed under the day they were received.
167:         Regex("\\bon\\s+(?:date\\s+)?(\\d{1,2}[-/][A-Za-z]{3,9}[-/]\\d{2,4})", I),
168:         Regex("\\bon\\s+(?:date\\s+)?(\\d{1,2}[-/]\\d{1,2}[-/]\\d{2,4})", I),
169:         Regex("\\bon\\s+(?:date\\s+)?(\\d{1,2}[A-Za-z]{3}\\d{2,4})", I),   // SBI "08Jul26"
170:         Regex("\\b(\\d{4}-\\d{2}-\\d{2})\\b"),
171:     )
172:     private val TIME = Regex("\\b(\\d{1,2}:\\d{2}(?::\\d{2})?)\\b")
173: 
174:     private val MONTHS = listOf(
175:         "jan", "feb", "mar", "apr", "may", "jun",
176:         "jul", "aug", "sep", "oct", "nov", "dec")
177: 
178:     /** Canonicalise a message before any pattern is applied.
179:      *
180:      * NFKC folds full-width and compatibility forms (ｒｓ, ￥) to ASCII, Indian
181:      * script digits are mapped, invisible marks are stripped, whitespace runs
182:      * collapse. Without this an ordinary message can fail to parse for
183:      * reasons nobody can see by reading it. */
184:     fun normalizeText(text: String?): String {
185:         if (text.isNullOrEmpty()) return ""
186:         var s = Normalizer.normalize(text, Normalizer.Form.NFKC)
187:         if (s.any { it in DIGIT_MAP }) {
188:             s = s.map { DIGIT_MAP[it] ?: it }.joinToString("")
189:         }
190:         s = INVISIBLE.replace(s, "")
191:         s = s.replace('\u00a0', ' ')
192:         return WS_RUN.replace(s, " ").trim()
193:     }
194: 
195:     /** A usable rupee amount, or null if the value is not one.
196:      *
197:      * Rejects NaN, both infinities, and anything outside (0, MAX_AMOUNT].
198:      * This exists because parsing a number accepts far more than money: a
199:      * 400-digit string becomes Infinity and compares as > 0, so it passed the
200:      * transaction gate. A stored Infinity then reached the analytics, where
201:      * (amount * 100) overflowed and permanently broke the dashboard — a
202:      * denial of service triggerable by anyone who can send the device an SMS. */
203:     fun safeAmount(value: Any?): Double? {
204:         val f = when (value) {
205:             null -> return null
206:             is Double -> value
207:             is Number -> value.toDouble()
208:             is String -> value.trim().replace(",", "").toDoubleOrNull() ?: return null
209:             else -> return null
210:         }
211:         if (f.isNaN() || f.isInfinite()) return null
212:         if (f <= 0.0 || f > MAX_AMOUNT) return null
213:         return f
214:     }
215: 
216:     private fun parseAmount(text: String): Double? {
217:         // Verb-anchored wins: it is unambiguously the transaction amount.
218:         VERB_AMOUNT.find(text)?.let { return safeAmount(it.groupValues[1]) }
219:         // Otherwise the first currency-marked amount that is NOT a balance.
220:         for (m in AMOUNT.findAll(text)) {
221:             val ctx = text.substring(maxOf(0, m.range.first - 24), m.range.first)
222:             if (BALANCE_CTX.containsMatchIn(ctx)) continue
223:             return safeAmount(m.groupValues[1])
224:         }
225:         return null
226:     }
227: 
228:     private fun parseDate(text: String): LocalDateTime? {
229:         var raw: String? = null
230:         for (rx in DATE_PATTERNS) {
231:             rx.find(text)?.let { raw = it.groupValues[1]; return@let }
232:             if (raw != null) break
233:         }
234:         val value = raw ?: return null
235:         val date = parseDateParts(value) ?: return null
236:         var hour = 0
237:         var minute = 0
238:         TIME.find(text)?.let {
239:             val parts = it.groupValues[1].split(":")
240:             hour = parts[0].toIntOrNull()?.coerceIn(0, 23) ?: 0
241:             minute = parts.getOrNull(1)?.toIntOrNull()?.coerceIn(0, 59) ?: 0
242:         }
243:         return LocalDateTime.of(date.year, date.monthValue, date.dayOfMonth, hour, minute)
244:     }
245: 
246:     /** Accepts every shape the bank corpus actually uses: 05-Jan-24,
247:      *  08/07/2026, 08Jul26, 2026-07-08. Written out rather than handed to a
248:      *  date library because the two-digit-year and no-separator forms need
249:      *  rules a formatter cannot express in one pattern. */
250:     private fun parseDateParts(raw: String): LocalDate? {
251:         fun year(y: Int) = if (y < 100) 2000 + y else y
252:         fun month(name: String): Int? {
253:             val idx = MONTHS.indexOf(name.lowercase().take(3))
254:             return if (idx >= 0) idx + 1 else null
255:         }
256:         Regex("^(\\d{4})-(\\d{2})-(\\d{2})$").find(raw)?.let { m ->
257:             return safeDate(m.groupValues[1].toInt(), m.groupValues[2].toInt(),
258:                             m.groupValues[3].toInt())
259:         }
260:         Regex("^(\\d{1,2})[-/]([A-Za-z]{3,9})[-/](\\d{2,4})$").find(raw)?.let { m ->
261:             val mm = month(m.groupValues[2]) ?: return null
262:             return safeDate(year(m.groupValues[3].toInt()), mm, m.groupValues[1].toInt())
263:         }
264:         Regex("^(\\d{1,2})[-/](\\d{1,2})[-/](\\d{2,4})$").find(raw)?.let { m ->
265:             return safeDate(year(m.groupValues[3].toInt()), m.groupValues[2].toInt(),
266:                             m.groupValues[1].toInt())
267:         }
268:         Regex("^(\\d{1,2})([A-Za-z]{3})(\\d{2,4})$").find(raw)?.let { m ->
269:             val mm = month(m.groupValues[2]) ?: return null
270:             return safeDate(year(m.groupValues[3].toInt()), mm, m.groupValues[1].toInt())
271:         }
272:         return null
273:     }
274: 
275:     private fun safeDate(y: Int, m: Int, d: Int): LocalDate? =
276:         try { LocalDate.of(y, m, d) } catch (e: Exception) { null }
277: 
278:     /** @param forKey read the payee the way the dedup key always has — see
279:      *  [ParsedSms.keyMerchant]. */
280:     private fun cleanMerchant(raw: String, forKey: Boolean = false): String? {
281:         var name = raw.trim(' ', '.', ',', '-', '/').substringBefore('@').trim()
282:         if (name.isEmpty()) return null
283:         // Reject pure digits and account fragments ("X1234", "919…").
284:         if (!Regex("[A-Za-z]{2}").containsMatchIn(name)) return null
285:         if (BAD_MERCHANT.matches(name)) return null
286:         if (!forKey && ACCOUNT_REF.containsMatchIn(name)) return null
287:         // A real payee is short. Anything sentence-like is scraped prose.
288:         if (name.split(Regex("\\s+")).size > 4) return null
289:         return name
290:     }
291: 
292:     private fun parseMerchant(text: String, type: String, forKey: Boolean = false): String? {
293:         val strategies = if (type == "expense")
294:             listOf(TO, UPI_PATH, PAYEE_CREDITED, FROM)
295:         else
296:             listOf(FROM, TO, UPI_PATH)
297:         for (rx in strategies) {
298:             val m = rx.find(text) ?: continue
299:             cleanMerchant(m.groupValues[1], forKey)?.let { return it }
300:         }
301:         return null
302:     }
303: 
304:     /** Parse a finance SMS. Never throws: an unparseable message comes back
305:      *  with matched = false, which is a normal outcome, not an error. */
306:     fun parse(text: String?): ParsedSms {
307:         val normalized = normalizeText(text)
308:         if (normalized.isEmpty()) return ParsedSms()
309: 
310:         val amount = parseAmount(normalized)
311:         val credited = CREDIT.containsMatchIn(normalized)
312:         val debitVerb = DEBIT.containsMatchIn(normalized)
313:         val refund = REFUND.containsMatchIn(normalized) && !DEBITED_NOW.containsMatchIn(normalized)
314:         val type = if (credited && (!debitVerb || refund)) "income" else "expense"
315:         // The direction parser 2026.1.0-kt chose, which decides the payee
316:         // pattern order and so the dedup key's payee — see keyMerchant.
317:         val keyType = if (credited && !debitVerb) "income" else "expense"
318: 
319:         // A real transaction needs ALL of: an amount, a money-movement verb,
320:         // no promo/request/pre-debit language, and positive account evidence.
321:         // The last condition is what keeps promotional and scam messages —
322:         // which happily carry amounts and even verbs — out of the ledger.
323:         // Everything a real transaction has, ignoring whether the wording is
324:         // suspicious. Kept separate from `matched` because the two answer
325:         // different questions, and collapsing them loses information the
326:         // quarantine screen needs.
327:         val looksTransactional = amount != null &&
328:             amount > 0 &&
329:             TXN_VERB.containsMatchIn(normalized) &&
330:             ACCOUNT_EVIDENCE.containsMatchIn(normalized)
331: 
332:         return ParsedSms(
333:             amount = amount,
334:             type = type,
335:             rawMerchant = parseMerchant(normalized, type),
336:             referenceNumber = REF.find(normalized)?.groupValues?.get(1),
337:             occurredAt = parseDate(normalized),
338:             // A real transaction ALSO has to be free of promo, request,
339:             // pre-debit and scam language.
340:             matched = looksTransactional && !NON_TXN.containsMatchIn(normalized),
341:             looksTransactional = looksTransactional,
342:             keyMerchant = parseMerchant(normalized, keyType, forKey = true),
343:         )
344:     }
345: }
346: 
347: data class ParsedSms(
348:     val amount: Double? = null,
349:     val type: String = "expense",
350:     val rawMerchant: String? = null,
351:     val referenceNumber: String? = null,
352:     val occurredAt: LocalDateTime? = null,
353:     val matched: Boolean = false,
354:     /** Has the shape of a transaction — amount, money-movement verb and
355:      *  account evidence — regardless of whether the wording is suspicious.
356:      *
357:      * A scam that says "Rs.9,999 debited... not you? call 9812345678" fails
358:      * `matched` on its scam vocabulary and is therefore never banked. But it
359:      * still LOOKS like a debit, and the person may genuinely have been
360:      * debited, so it belongs in front of them rather than silently dropped.
361:      * A promo with an amount and no verb has neither flag and is simply
362:      * noise. */
363:     val looksTransactional: Boolean = false,
364:     /** The payee as the dedup key reads it: parser 2026.1.0-kt's reading,
365:      *  kept even where [rawMerchant] has since been corrected.
366:      *
367:      * The inbox scan re-parses ninety days of messages on every launch and
368:      * recognises what is already banked by recomputing its dedup key. A key
369:      * that moved with every payee fix would stop matching the rows a phone
370:      * already holds, and the next scan would file each of them again. */
371:     val keyMerchant: String? = rawMerchant,
372: )

FILE core/src/main/kotlin/com/jeevavibeapp/spendwise/core/Pipeline.kt
1: package com.jeevavibeapp.spendwise.core
2: 
3: import java.time.LocalDateTime
4: 
5: /**
6:  * What happens to an incoming SMS, as a pure decision — and what happens
7:  * when the user answers the question a low-confidence capture asked.
8:  *
9:  * This sequence is the most consequential code in the app: it decides what
10:  * lands in someone's ledger and what gets held back. Keeping it here — with
11:  * lookups injected rather than a database reached into — means the whole
12:  * flow can be tested on a JVM in milliseconds, including the cases that are
13:  * awkward to reproduce on a device, like a spoofed header or a blocked
14:  * sender.
15:  */
16: object Pipeline {
17: 
18:     sealed class Outcome {
19:         /** A real transaction. `status` is what the ledger should record. */
20:         data class Capture(
21:             val parsed: ParsedSms,
22:             val merchantName: String?,
23:             val categoryId: String?,
24:             val confidence: Int,
25:             val status: String,
26:             val dedupKey: String,
27:             val needsCategory: Boolean,
28:         ) : Outcome()
29: 
30:         /** Held for the user to judge. Never silently discarded. */
31:         data class Hold(val parsed: ParsedSms, val verdict: RiskAssessment,
32:                         val reason: String) : Outcome()
33: 
34:         /** Not a transaction at all: an OTP, a promo, a personal message. */
35:         data object NotFinancial : Outcome()
36:     }
37: 
38:     /** Name, category and confidence for a payee, from whatever evidence exists. */
39:     data class Resolution(val merchantName: String?, val categoryId: String?,
40:                           val confidence: Int)
41: 
42:     const val STATUS_CONFIRMED = "confirmed"
43:     const val STATUS_PENDING = "pending_confirmation"
44:     const val STATUS_REVIEW = "needs_review"
45: 
46:     /**
47:      * @param lookupSender what is stored about this sender, or null if new
48:      * @param resolve      name/category/confidence for a raw payee string
49:      * @param autoAt       confidence at or above which we save without asking
50:      * @param confirmAt    confidence at or above which we ask rather than bury
51:      */
52:     fun ingest(
53:         sender: String?,
54:         body: String,
55:         now: LocalDateTime,
56:         lookupSender: (String) -> SenderRegistry?,
57:         resolve: (rawMerchant: String?, amount: Double?, at: LocalDateTime) -> Resolution,
58:         autoAt: Int = 80,
59:         confirmAt: Int = 50,
60:     ): Outcome {
61:         // 1. Parse FIRST, and judge the sender second. The parse gate is
62:         //    entirely generic on purpose: knowing the sender may enrich a
63:         //    message that already looks like a transaction, but it must never
64:         //    be able to MAKE something a transaction, or a spoofed header
65:         //    could inject rows into the ledger.
66:         val parsed = Parsing.parse(body)
67:         if (parsed.amount == null || !parsed.looksTransactional) return Outcome.NotFinancial
68: 
69:         // 2. Now the sender, with whatever the app has learned about it.
70:         val registry = sender?.let { lookupSender(Senders.normalizeSender(it)) }
71:         val verdict = Senders.assess(sender, body, registry)
72:         if (verdict.action == Senders.ACTION_QUARANTINE) {
73:             return Outcome.Hold(parsed, verdict, Senders.explain(verdict))
74:         }
75: 
76:         // A message shaped like a debit but worded like a scam or a promo is
77:         // never banked. Where it goes depends on which: scam wording means
78:         // someone is targeting this person and they should see it, while a
79:         // pre-debit notice or an offer is just noise.
80:         if (!parsed.matched) {
81:             return if (verdict.risk > 0) Outcome.Hold(parsed, verdict, Senders.explain(verdict))
82:                    else Outcome.NotFinancial
83:         }
84: 
85:         // 3. Resolve the payee, then let the sender's risk pull confidence
86:         //    DOWN — never up. An unverified sender should make the app ask
87:         //    more often, not less.
88:         val at = parsed.occurredAt ?: now
89:         val resolution = resolve(parsed.rawMerchant, parsed.amount, at)
90:         val confidence = (resolution.confidence + verdict.confidenceDelta).coerceIn(0, 100)
91: 
92:         val status = when (Engine.decide(confidence, autoAt, confirmAt)) {
93:             Engine.DECISION_AUTO -> STATUS_CONFIRMED
94:             Engine.DECISION_CONFIRM -> STATUS_PENDING
95:             else -> STATUS_REVIEW
96:         }
97: 
98:         return Outcome.Capture(
99:             parsed = parsed,
100:             merchantName = resolution.merchantName,
101:             categoryId = resolution.categoryId,
102:             confidence = confidence,
103:             status = status,
104:             dedupKey = dedupKey(parsed, at),
105:             needsCategory = resolution.categoryId == null,
106:         )
107:     }
108: 
109:     /** A person said so, and no scored guess outranks that. Written onto the
110:      *  row so the next thing to read it — a resolution, a report, an export —
111:      *  cannot treat a settled payee as still doubtful. */
112:     const val CONFIRMED_CONFIDENCE = 100
113: 
114:     /** What confirming writes onto a capture, plus how the engine should
115:      *  learn it. */
116:     data class Confirmation(
117:         val merchantName: String,
118:         val categoryId: String?,
119:         val status: String,
120:         val confidence: Int,
121:         /** The user renamed the payee rather than agreeing with it. Scoring
122:          *  weighs corrections against confirmations, so recording a rename as
123:          *  agreement would make the engine more confident about the very
124:          *  guess it just got wrong. */
125:         val isCorrection: Boolean,
126:     )
127: 
128:     /**
129:      * The user naming the payee on a capture the app was not sure about —
130:      * the half of the loop that closes it.
131:      *
132:      * A capture below [ingest]'s auto threshold lands as pending or
133:      * needs_review and stays there until this runs; nothing else in the app
134:      * moves a stored row to confirmed.
135:      *
136:      * @param storedName    the payee currently on the row, if any
137:      * @param storedCategoryId kept when the caller names no category, so
138:      *   confirming a name cannot quietly uncategorise the row
139:      * @return null when there is no name to file. Blank is the caller's
140:      *   signal to leave the row exactly as it is: writing an empty payee over
141:      *   what the bank message said would destroy the only evidence there is.
142:      */
143:     fun confirm(
144:         storedName: String?,
145:         storedCategoryId: String?,
146:         name: String,
147:         categoryId: String? = null,
148:     ): Confirmation? {
149:         val clean = name.trim()
150:         if (clean.isEmpty()) return null
151:         return Confirmation(
152:             merchantName = clean,
153:             categoryId = categoryId ?: storedCategoryId,
154:             status = STATUS_CONFIRMED,
155:             confidence = CONFIRMED_CONFIDENCE,
156:             // Case alone is not a correction: accepting "swiggy" against a
157:             // stored "Swiggy" is the user agreeing, and counting it as a
158:             // rename would penalise a mapping they just endorsed.
159:             isCorrection = !storedName.isNullOrBlank() &&
160:                 !storedName.equals(clean, ignoreCase = true),
161:         )
162:     }
163: 
164:     /**
165:      * Identity of a payment, independent of how it was worded.
166:      *
167:      * Several banks send both an SMS and a UPI confirmation for one payment,
168:      * and the app is launched repeatedly, rescanning the same inbox. Amount +
169:      * folded merchant + reference + DAY is stable across all of that, while
170:      * still separating two genuinely different coffees on the same morning
171:      * once a reference number is present.
172:      */
173:     fun dedupKey(parsed: ParsedSms, at: LocalDateTime): String = listOf(
174:         parsed.amount?.toString().orEmpty(),
175:         Engine.normalizeMerchant(parsed.keyMerchant),
176:         parsed.referenceNumber.orEmpty(),
177:         at.toLocalDate().toString(),
178:     ).joinToString("|")
179: }

FILE core/src/main/kotlin/com/jeevavibeapp/spendwise/core/Engine.kt
1: package com.jeevavibeapp.spendwise.core
2: 
3: import java.time.LocalDateTime
4: import kotlin.math.abs
5: import kotlin.math.max
6: import kotlin.math.min
7: import kotlin.math.roundToInt
8: 
9: /**
10:  * Merchant resolution, confidence scoring and learning. Pure Kotlin.
11:  *
12:  * Confidence adds to 100:
13:  *   past mapping 40, amount pattern 20, category pattern 15,
14:  *   correction history 15, time pattern 10.
15:  *
16:  * Nothing here touches a database. The caller supplies the learning rows and
17:  * decides what to persist, which is what lets the whole scoring model be
18:  * tested on a JVM in milliseconds instead of through an emulator.
19:  */
20: object Engine {
21: 
22:     const val W_PAST_MAPPING = 40.0
23:     const val W_AMOUNT = 20.0
24:     const val W_CATEGORY = 15.0
25:     const val W_CORRECTION = 15.0
26:     const val W_TIME = 10.0
27:     private const val FULL_TRUST = 5.0
28: 
29:     const val DECISION_AUTO = "auto_saved"
30:     const val DECISION_CONFIRM = "confirmation_required"
31:     const val DECISION_MANUAL = "manual_required"
32: 
33:     const val SEED_CONFIDENCE = 90
34: 
35:     private val NOISE_TOKENS = setOf(
36:         "UPI", "VPA", "P2M", "P2A", "POS", "NEFT", "IMPS", "RTGS", "ACH",
37:         "PVT", "LTD", "LIMITED", "PRIVATE", "AND", "THE",
38:         "PAYMENTS", "PAYMENT", "INDIA", "ONLINE", "RETAIL", "STORES", "STORE")
39: 
40:     private val NOISE = Regex("[^A-Z0-9& ]+")
41:     private val LONG_DIGITS = Regex("\\b\\d{4,}\\b")
42:     private val GLUED_DIGITS = Regex("(?<=[A-Z])\\d{1,3}\\b")
43: 
44:     /** Fold every spelling of a merchant onto one key, so all of a merchant's
45:      *  handle variants share the learning the user did once. */
46:     fun normalizeMerchant(raw: String?): String {
47:         if (raw.isNullOrBlank()) return ""
48:         var s = raw.uppercase().trim().substringBefore('@')
49:         s = s.replace('/', ' ').replace('-', ' ').replace('_', ' ').replace('.', ' ')
50:         s = LONG_DIGITS.replace(s, " ")
51:         // VPA-style digit suffixes glued to the name: SWIGGY8 -> SWIGGY.
52:         s = GLUED_DIGITS.replace(s, "")
53:         s = NOISE.replace(s, " ")
54:         val tokens = s.split(" ").filter { it.isNotEmpty() && it !in NOISE_TOKENS }
55:             .toMutableList()
56:         // Trailing pure-digit tokens are references, not names.
57:         while (tokens.isNotEmpty() && tokens.last().all { it.isDigit() }) {
58:             tokens.removeAt(tokens.size - 1)
59:         }
60:         return tokens.joinToString(" ").trim()
61:     }
62: 
63:     /** Built-in Indian merchants, so a fresh install recognises the obvious
64:      *  ones before any learning exists. */
65:     val SEED_MERCHANTS: Map<String, Pair<String, String>> = mapOf(
66:         "SWIGGY" to ("Swiggy" to "Food & Dining"),
67:         "ZOMATO" to ("Zomato" to "Food & Dining"),
68:         "DOMINOS" to ("Dominos" to "Food & Dining"),
69:         "KFC" to ("KFC" to "Food & Dining"),
70:         "MCDONALD" to ("McDonalds" to "Food & Dining"),
71:         "MCDONALDS" to ("McDonalds" to "Food & Dining"),
72:         "STARBUCKS" to ("Starbucks" to "Food & Dining"),
73:         "PIZZAHUT" to ("Pizza Hut" to "Food & Dining"),
74:         "BLINKIT" to ("Blinkit" to "Groceries"),
75:         "ZEPTO" to ("Zepto" to "Groceries"),
76:         "BIGBASKET" to ("BigBasket" to "Groceries"),
77:         "DMART" to ("DMart" to "Groceries"),
78:         "JIOMART" to ("JioMart" to "Groceries"),
79:         "INSTAMART" to ("Swiggy Instamart" to "Groceries"),
80:         "AMAZON" to ("Amazon" to "Shopping"),
81:         "FLIPKART" to ("Flipkart" to "Shopping"),
82:         "MYNTRA" to ("Myntra" to "Shopping"),
83:         "AJIO" to ("Ajio" to "Shopping"),
84:         "MEESHO" to ("Meesho" to "Shopping"),
85:         "NYKAA" to ("Nykaa" to "Shopping"),
86:         "UBER" to ("Uber" to "Transport"),
87:         "OLA" to ("Ola" to "Transport"),
88:         "RAPIDO" to ("Rapido" to "Transport"),
89:         "IRCTC" to ("IRCTC" to "Transport"),
90:         "REDBUS" to ("RedBus" to "Transport"),
91:         "INDIGO" to ("IndiGo" to "Transport"),
92:         "JIO" to ("Jio" to "Bills & Utilities"),
93:         "AIRTEL" to ("Airtel" to "Bills & Utilities"),
94:         "VODAFONE" to ("Vi" to "Bills & Utilities"),
95:         "BSNL" to ("BSNL" to "Bills & Utilities"),
96:         "TATAPOWER" to ("Tata Power" to "Bills & Utilities"),
97:         "BESCOM" to ("BESCOM" to "Bills & Utilities"),
98:         "NETFLIX" to ("Netflix" to "Entertainment"),
99:         "HOTSTAR" to ("Disney+ Hotstar" to "Entertainment"),
100:         "SPOTIFY" to ("Spotify" to "Entertainment"),
101:         "PRIMEVIDEO" to ("Prime Video" to "Entertainment"),
102:         "BOOKMYSHOW" to ("BookMyShow" to "Entertainment"),
103:         "SONYLIV" to ("SonyLIV" to "Entertainment"),
104:         "APOLLO" to ("Apollo Pharmacy" to "Health"),
105:         "PHARMEASY" to ("PharmEasy" to "Health"),
106:         "NETMEDS" to ("Netmeds" to "Health"),
107:         "PRACTO" to ("Practo" to "Health"),
108:     )
109: 
110:     fun seedLookup(normalized: String): Pair<String, String>? {
111:         if (normalized.isBlank()) return null
112:         SEED_MERCHANTS[normalized.replace(" ", "")]?.let { return it }
113:         return SEED_MERCHANTS[normalized.split(" ").first()]
114:     }
115: 
116:     private fun scorePast(row: LearningRow): Double {
117:         val strength = row.confirmationCount + 0.5 * row.sampleCount - row.correctionCount
118:         return W_PAST_MAPPING * max(0.0, min(1.0, strength / FULL_TRUST))
119:     }
120: 
121:     private fun scoreAmount(row: LearningRow, amount: Double?): Double {
122:         if (amount == null || row.sampleCount <= 0) return 0.0
123:         val tolerance = max(max(row.avgAmount * 0.25, (row.amountMax - row.amountMin) / 2.0), 1.0)
124:         var closeness = max(0.0, 1.0 - abs(amount - row.avgAmount) / (tolerance * 2.0))
125:         if (amount in row.amountMin..row.amountMax) closeness = max(closeness, 0.6)
126:         return W_AMOUNT * closeness
127:     }
128: 
129:     private fun scoreCategory(row: LearningRow, categoryId: String?): Double {
130:         if (row.categoryId == null) return 0.0
131:         if (categoryId == null) return W_CATEGORY * 0.5
132:         return if (row.categoryId == categoryId) W_CATEGORY else 0.0
133:     }
134: 
135:     private fun scoreCorrection(row: LearningRow): Double {
136:         val denom = row.confirmationCount + row.correctionCount + 1
137:         return W_CORRECTION * ((row.confirmationCount + 1).toDouble() / denom)
138:     }
139: 
140:     private fun scoreTime(row: LearningRow, occurredAt: LocalDateTime?): Double {
141:         val hist = row.hourHistogram
142:         if (occurredAt == null || hist.isEmpty() || hist.sum() <= 0) return 0.0
143:         val hour = occurredAt.hour % 24
144:         val peak = max(hist.max(), 1)
145:         return W_TIME * (if (hour < hist.size) hist[hour].toDouble() / peak else 0.0)
146:     }
147: 
148:     fun score(row: LearningRow, amount: Double?, categoryId: String?,
149:               occurredAt: LocalDateTime?): Breakdown {
150:         val past = scorePast(row)
151:         val amt = scoreAmount(row, amount)
152:         val cat = scoreCategory(row, categoryId)
153:         val corr = scoreCorrection(row)
154:         val tm = scoreTime(row, occurredAt)
155:         val total = max(0.0, min(100.0, past + amt + cat + corr + tm)).roundToInt()
156:         return Breakdown(past, amt, cat, corr, tm, total)
157:     }
158: 
159:     fun decide(total: Int, auto: Int, confirm: Int): String = when {
160:         total >= auto -> DECISION_AUTO
161:         total >= confirm -> DECISION_CONFIRM
162:         else -> DECISION_MANUAL
163:     }
164: 
165:     /** Jaccard overlap between token sets, so "SWIGGY INSTAMART" still finds
166:      *  the learning the user already did on "SWIGGY". */
167:     fun tokenOverlap(a: String, b: String): Double {
168:         val ta = a.split(" ").filter { it.isNotEmpty() }.toSet()
169:         val tb = b.split(" ").filter { it.isNotEmpty() }.toSet()
170:         if (ta.isEmpty() || tb.isEmpty()) return 0.0
171:         return ta.intersect(tb).size.toDouble() / ta.union(tb).size
172:     }
173: 
174:     /** Confidence from the mapping alone, with no incoming transaction to
175:      *  compare against — what the ledger shows for an already-learned payee. */
176:     fun baselineConfidence(row: LearningRow): Int {
177:         val past = scorePast(row)
178:         val correction = scoreCorrection(row)
179:         val category = if (row.categoryId != null) W_CATEGORY else 0.0
180:         return max(0.0, min(100.0, past + correction + category)).roundToInt()
181:     }
182: 
183:     /** Why a prediction scored what it did. A suggestion the user cannot
184:      *  interrogate is one they will eventually stop trusting. */
185:     fun explain(b: Breakdown, row: LearningRow?, seeded: Boolean = false): List<String> {
186:         if (seeded) return listOf("Well-known Indian merchant (built-in)")
187:         val out = mutableListOf<String>()
188:         if (row != null) {
189:             if (row.confirmationCount > 0)
190:                 out += "You confirmed this payee ${row.confirmationCount} time" +
191:                        (if (row.confirmationCount == 1) "" else "s")
192:             if (row.correctionCount > 0)
193:                 out += "Corrected ${row.correctionCount} time" +
194:                        (if (row.correctionCount == 1) "" else "s")
195:         }
196:         if (b.amountPattern >= W_AMOUNT * 0.5) out += "The amount matches what you usually pay here"
197:         if (b.categoryPattern >= W_CATEGORY) out += "Same category as before"
198:         if (b.timePattern >= W_TIME * 0.5) out += "You usually pay here around this time"
199:         if (out.isEmpty()) out += "No history for this payee yet"
200:         return out
201:     }
202: }
203: 
204: /** One learned raw-name -> merchant mapping. */
205: data class LearningRow(
206:     val rawName: String,
207:     val merchantName: String,
208:     val categoryId: String? = null,
209:     val confirmationCount: Int = 0,
210:     val correctionCount: Int = 0,
211:     val sampleCount: Int = 0,
212:     val avgAmount: Double = 0.0,
213:     val amountMin: Double = 0.0,
214:     val amountMax: Double = 0.0,
215:     val hourHistogram: List<Int> = emptyList(),
216: )
217: 
218: data class Breakdown(
219:     val pastMapping: Double,
220:     val amountPattern: Double,
221:     val categoryPattern: Double,
222:     val correctionHistory: Double,
223:     val timePattern: Double,
224:     val total: Int,
225: )

FILE core/src/main/kotlin/com/jeevavibeapp/spendwise/core/Fraud.kt
1: package com.jeevavibeapp.spendwise.core
2: 
3: import java.time.LocalDateTime
4: import java.time.format.DateTimeFormatter
5: import java.time.temporal.ChronoUnit
6: import java.util.Locale
7: import kotlin.math.abs
8: import kotlin.math.roundToLong
9: import kotlin.math.sqrt
10: 
11: /**
12:  * Fraud detection over the user's own history: the five questions a ledger
13:  * can answer about a charge the moment it lands.
14:  *
15:  * Distinct from [Insights.anomalies], which is a monthly report section about
16:  * large charges at a merchant the user already knows. That cannot see a card
17:  * charged twice in four minutes, cannot see a payee that has never appeared
18:  * before, and has nowhere to record that the user looked and it was fine.
19:  * This can, and every signal it raises is about ONE transaction at the moment
20:  * it is filed.
21:  *
22:  * Pure, like the rest of :core — the caller supplies the ledger and stores
23:  * the result. Every threshold here is a constant rather than a literal buried
24:  * in a condition, because they are the whole behaviour and a test that cannot
25:  * name them cannot pin them.
26:  */
27: object Fraud {
28: 
29:     const val DUPLICATE_WINDOW_MIN = 10L
30:     const val OUTLIER_SIGMA = 3.0
31:     /** An excess smaller than this is noise whatever the sigma says. The same
32:      *  reasoning as Insights.ANOMALY_FLOOR: on a ledger of Rs.50 chai the
33:      *  arithmetic will happily call Rs.62 a four-sigma event. */
34:     const val OUTLIER_FLOOR = 300.0
35:     const val ABNORMAL_DAY_FACTOR = 3.0
36:     const val NEW_MERCHANT_MULTIPLE = 2.0
37:     /** Charges needed before "usual" means anything. Under this every
38:      *  detector that compares against the user's own distribution stays
39:      *  silent rather than guessing from four numbers. */
40:     const val MIN_HISTORY = 8
41: 
42:     /**
43:      * How recent a charge has to be for an alert about it to be worth
44:      * raising at all — see [worthAlerting].
45:      *
46:      * An alert exists to be acted on, and those windows close: a bank wants
47:      * to hear about a double charge this week, not one from the spring. It is
48:      * also what keeps a first launch usable. The inbox scan backfills ninety
49:      * days of messages at once, so without this the first thing a new user
50:      * sees is a tab reading "Alerts (23)" over purchases they have already
51:      * lived through — which teaches them to ignore it before it has ever
52:      * carried anything live.
53:      */
54:     const val ALERT_WINDOW_DAYS = 30L
55: 
56:     const val DUPLICATE = "duplicate"
57:     const val OUTLIER = "high_value_outlier"
58:     const val OVER_LIMIT = "high_value_limit"
59:     const val ABNORMAL_DAY = "abnormal_spend"
60:     const val NEW_MERCHANT = "unusual_merchant"
61: 
62:     const val LOW = "low"
63:     const val MEDIUM = "medium"
64:     const val HIGH = "high"
65: 
66:     private val DAY = DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern("d MMM", Locale.ENGLISH)
67: 
68:     /** Half a paisa: the same tolerance Analytics uses to call two amounts
69:      *  equal, because a double that came back through SQLite is not reliably
70:      *  bit-identical to the one that went in. */
71:     private const val AMOUNT_EPS = 0.005
72: 
73:     /**
74:      * Every signal [tx] raises against [ledger].
75:      *
76:      * [ledger] is the rest of the user's transactions — soft-deleted rows
77:      * excluded by the caller, [tx] itself tolerated and filtered out here so
78:      * a caller that passes the whole ledger after inserting gets the same
79:      * answer as one that passes it before.
80:      *
81:      * [highValueLimit] is the amount the user typed on the settings screen.
82:      * Null or zero means they set none, which is not the same as zero.
83:      */
84:     fun evaluate(tx: Tx, ledger: List<Tx>, highValueLimit: Double? = null): List<FraudAlert> {
85:         if (tx.type != "expense") return emptyList()
86: 
87:         val others = ledger.filter { it.id != tx.id }
88:         val amount = tx.amount
89:         val name = tx.merchantName?.takeIf { it.isNotBlank() }
90:         val day = tx.occurredAt.toLocalDate()
91:         val out = mutableListOf<FraudAlert>()
92: 
93:         // 1. The same amount at the same payee, minutes apart. The Python
94:         //    original compares amounts only, with no type filter, so a salary
95:         //    credit landing within ten minutes of a purchase of the same size
96:         //    reported the purchase as a duplicate charge — and a self
97:         //    transfer, which is a debit and a credit of exactly equal amounts
98:         //    close together, was guaranteed to trip it. Expenses only here.
99:         val duplicate = others.any {
100:             it.type == "expense" &&
101:                 sameAmount(it.amount, amount) &&
102:                 abs(ChronoUnit.MINUTES.between(it.occurredAt, tx.occurredAt)) <=
103:                 DUPLICATE_WINDOW_MIN &&
104:                 (name == null || it.merchantName == name)
105:         }
106:         if (duplicate) {
107:             out += FraudAlert(
108:                 key = "$DUPLICATE:${tx.id}", txId = tx.id, kind = DUPLICATE, severity = MEDIUM,
109:                 message = "Possible duplicate: ${Insights.money(amount)}" +
110:                     (if (name != null) " at $name" else "") +
111:                     ", twice within $DUPLICATE_WINDOW_MIN minutes.")
112:         }
113: 
114:         val history = others.filter { it.type == "expense" }.map { it.amount }
115:         val enoughHistory = history.size >= MIN_HISTORY
116:         val mean = if (history.isEmpty()) 0.0 else history.average()
117: 
118:         // 2. Far outside the user's own distribution.
119:         if (enoughHistory) {
120:             val spread = stdev(history, mean)
121:             // The Python original substitutes 1.0 for a zero standard
122:             // deviation, which turns a ledger of identical amounts into a
123:             // machine for manufacturing high-severity alerts: eight charges
124:             // of Rs.50 make any Rs.54 charge a four-sigma event. No spread
125:             // means there is no distribution to be an outlier of, so the
126:             // detector stays quiet instead.
127:             if (spread > 0 && amount - mean >= OUTLIER_FLOOR) {
128:                 val sigma = (amount - mean) / spread
129:                 if (sigma >= OUTLIER_SIGMA) {
130:                     out += FraudAlert(
131:                         key = "$OUTLIER:${tx.id}", txId = tx.id, kind = OUTLIER, severity = HIGH,
132:                         message = "${Insights.money(amount)} is " +
133:                             "${Math.round(sigma * 10) / 10.0} standard deviations above " +
134:                             "your average charge of ${Insights.money(mean)}.")
135:                 }
136:             }
137:         }
138: 
139:         // 3. The line the user drew themselves. Not gated on history: they
140:         //    stated the number, and an app that ignores it until it has eight
141:         //    other charges to compare against has simply not honoured it.
142:         if (highValueLimit != null && highValueLimit > 0 && amount + AMOUNT_EPS >= highValueLimit) {
143:             out += FraudAlert(
144:                 key = "$OVER_LIMIT:${tx.id}", txId = tx.id, kind = OVER_LIMIT, severity = HIGH,
145:                 message = "${Insights.money(amount)} is at or above the " +
146:                     "${Insights.money(highValueLimit)} alert amount you set.")
147:         }
148: 
149:         // 4. A day far heavier than the user's own average day.
150:         if (enoughHistory) {
151:             val dayTotal = amount + others
152:                 .filter { it.type == "expense" && it.occurredAt.toLocalDate() == day }
153:                 .sumOf { it.amount }
154:             val dailyAverage = others
155:                 .filter { it.type == "expense" && it.occurredAt.toLocalDate() < day }
156:                 .groupBy { it.occurredAt.toLocalDate() }
157:                 .map { (_, rows) -> rows.sumOf { it.amount } }
158:                 .let { if (it.isEmpty()) 0.0 else it.average() }
159:             if (dailyAverage > 0 && dayTotal >= dailyAverage * ABNORMAL_DAY_FACTOR) {
160:                 // Keyed by the DAY, not by this transaction. The claim is
161:                 // about the day, and the Python original re-raises it for
162:                 // every further charge that day — so one heavy Saturday
163:                 // arrives as six identical alerts saying the same sentence.
164:                 out += FraudAlert(
165:                     key = "$ABNORMAL_DAY:$day", txId = tx.id, kind = ABNORMAL_DAY,
166:                     severity = MEDIUM,
167:                     message = "${Insights.money(dayTotal)} spent on ${day.format(DAY)}, " +
168:                         "against a daily average of ${Insights.money(dailyAverage)}.")
169:             }
170:         }
171: 
172:         // 5. A payee that has never appeared before, for a large amount.
173:         if (name != null && enoughHistory && amount > mean * NEW_MERCHANT_MULTIPLE) {
174:             if (others.none { it.merchantName == name }) {
175:                 out += FraudAlert(
176:                     key = "$NEW_MERCHANT:${tx.id}", txId = tx.id, kind = NEW_MERCHANT,
177:                     severity = LOW,
178:                     message = "First charge at $name, and ${Insights.money(amount)} is well " +
179:                         "above your average charge of ${Insights.money(mean)}.")
180:             }
181:         }
182: 
183:         return out.sortedByDescending { rank(it.severity) }
184:     }
185: 
186:     /**
187:      * Whether a charge is recent enough to raise alerts at all.
188:      *
189:      * Separate from [evaluate] because it answers a different question: that
190:      * one asks whether a charge looks wrong, this asks whether saying so is
191:      * still of any use. Callers that file transactions ask this first.
192:      *
193:      * No upper bound. A charge dated ahead of the clock — a bank's timestamp,
194:      * a phone in the wrong timezone — is the one thing that is certainly not
195:      * stale.
196:      */
197:     fun worthAlerting(occurredAt: LocalDateTime, now: LocalDateTime): Boolean =
198:         !occurredAt.toLocalDate().isBefore(now.toLocalDate().minusDays(ALERT_WINDOW_DAYS))
199: 
200:     /** High first. Exposed because the screen sorts a mixed list of stored
201:      *  alerts and cannot re-derive this from the severity string. */
202:     fun rank(severity: String): Int = when (severity) {
203:         HIGH -> 3
204:         MEDIUM -> 2
205:         else -> 1
206:     }
207: 
208:     private fun sameAmount(a: Double, b: Double): Boolean =
209:         (a * 100).roundToLong() == (b * 100).roundToLong()
210: 
211:     private fun stdev(values: List<Double>, mean: Double): Double {
212:         if (values.size < 2) return 0.0
213:         return sqrt(values.sumOf { val d = it - mean; d * d } / values.size)
214:     }
215: }
216: 
217: /**
218:  * One raised signal.
219:  *
220:  * [key] is what makes raising it twice impossible: it is derived from what
221:  * the claim is ABOUT — this transaction, or this day — so a re-ingest, a
222:  * rescan or a second charge on an already-flagged day cannot produce a second
223:  * copy. Storage enforces it with a unique index; nothing has to remember to
224:  * check.
225:  *
226:  * No JSON details blob. The Python original stores one and no screen has ever
227:  * read it: the message already carries every number that produced the claim.
228:  */
229: data class FraudAlert(
230:     val key: String,
231:     val txId: String,
232:     val kind: String,
233:     val severity: String,
234:     val message: String,
235: )

1: package com.jeevavibeapp.spendwise.core
2: 
3: import java.nio.ByteBuffer
4: import java.nio.charset.CodingErrorAction
5: import java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets
6: import java.time.LocalDate
7: import java.time.LocalDateTime

9: /**
10:  * Full-fidelity backup and restore, entirely on the device.
11:  *
12:  * The CSV export was never a backup. It is a report: it drops merchant
13:  * links, learning, category budgets, sender trust and every id, so restoring
14:  * from it would produce a ledger that looks similar and behaves differently.
15:  * For an app whose whole premise is that your data lives on your phone and
16:  * nowhere else, "your phone is the only copy" is not a feature — a lost
17:  * phone is a lost ledger, and there is no server to recover it from.
18:  *
19:  * So: a single JSON file the user holds. It carries the parts of the ledger
20:  * that mean something — transactions, categories, merchants, learned
21:  * merchant mappings, sender trust and preferences.
22:  *
23:  * Deliberately NOT included: raw SMS bodies, quarantined messages and parse
24:  * misses. They are the most sensitive text on the device and they are
25:  * recoverable — the inbox is still there and rescanning rebuilds them. A
26:  * backup file that contains a year of bank messages is a much worse thing to
27:  * lose than one that does not. For the same reason a captured transaction
28:  * carries no sender header either: the sender registry is backed up whole,
29:  * so the per-row copy adds nothing a restore needs, and a field that is not
30:  * in the file is a field that cannot leak out of it.
31:  *
32:  * Two rules shape everything here:
33:  *
34:  *  * A restore must never half-happen. This file therefore restores nothing;
35:  *    it produces a [Plan] — a pure, fully validated description of what to
36:  *    write. A damaged file yields a rejection and no plan at all, so there
37:  *    is no state in which half a backup has been applied. The caller writes
38:  *    a plan inside ONE database transaction, in the order the plan lists
39:  *    them (categories, merchants, learning, transactions, senders), so that
40:  *    a row never lands before the category it points at.
41:  *  * A restore must never trust the file. It is a document the user can open
42:  *    in a text editor, so every row is validated on the way in with the same
43:  *    rules the SMS and manual paths use: amounts go through
44:  *    [Parsing.safeAmount], thresholds are clamped, and a row that fails is
45:  *    dropped on its own rather than failing the restore.
46:  */
47: object Backup {

144:     /**

146:      *
147:      * [dropped] counts the rows the file contained but this build refused,
148:      * per section, so a restore can tell the user that 3 of 412 transactions

151:     data class Document(
152:         val createdAt: String,
153:         val categories: List<Category> = emptyList(),
154:         val merchants: List<Merchant> = emptyList(),

180:          *  file rather than the name of whatever went wrong. */
181:         data class Rejected(val reason: String) : Read()

184:     /**
185:      * The keys already in this ledger. Merging skips anything matching one
186:      * of them, which is what makes restoring the same file twice a no-op the
187:      * second time.
188:      *
189:      * The natural keys are here as well as the ids because the database
190:      * refuses a duplicate category name, merchant name, learned raw name,
191:      * sender or dedup key whatever id it arrives under — a plan that
192:      * promised to add such a row would be lying about what will happen.
193:      */
194:     data class Existing(
195:         val transactionIds: Set<String> = emptySet(),
196:         val dedupKeys: Set<String> = emptySet(),
197:         val categoryIds: Set<String> = emptySet(),
198:         val categoryNames: Set<String> = emptySet(),
199:         val merchantIds: Set<String> = emptySet(),
200:         val merchantNames: Set<String> = emptySet(),

203:         val senderIds: Set<String> = emptySet(),
204:         val senderNames: Set<String> = emptySet(),
205:     )

209:      *
210:      * [clearFirst] is the "new phone" path: delete this ledger's rows before
211:      * inserting. It is the only mode that can lose data, so the UI asks for
212:      * it explicitly. The lists are already in insertion order.
213:      */

224:     ) {
225:         val totalAdded: Int get() = added.values.sum()
226:         val totalSkipped: Int get() = skipped.values.sum()
